<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

$( 24 \div 3 ) + ( 12 \div 12 ) = \boxed{\phantom{5555}}$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= ( 24 \div 3 ) + ( 12 \div 12 ) \\\\
&= \dfrac{24}{3} + \dfrac{12}{12} \\\\
&= \dfrac{8 \times 3}{3} + \dfrac{12}{12} \\\\
&= \dfrac{8 \times \cancel{3}}{\cancel{3}} + \dfrac{\cancel{12}}{\cancel{12}} \\\\
&= 8 + 1 \\\\
&= 9
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$9$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
&= ( 24 \div 3 ) + ( 12 \div 12 ) \\\\
&= \dfrac{24}{3} + \dfrac{12}{12} \\\\
&= \dfrac{8 \times 3}{3} + \dfrac{12}{12} \\\\
&= \dfrac{8 \times \cancel{3}}{\cancel{3}} + \dfrac{\cancel{12}}{\cancel{12}} \\\\
&= 8 + 1 \\\\
&= 9
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

</div>

</code></pre>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

$125 \times 4 = 1000 \div \boxed{\phantom{5555}}$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
125 \times 4 &= 1000 \div a \\\\
125 \times 4 &= \dfrac{1000}{a} \\\\
500          &= \dfrac{1000}{a} \\\\
a            &= \dfrac{1000}{500}  \\\\
a            &= \dfrac{100 \times 10}{500}  \\\\
a            &= \dfrac{\cancel{500} \times 2}{\cancel{500}}  \\\\
a            &= 2
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$2$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$

a            &= \dfrac{1000}{500}  \\\\
a            &= \dfrac{100 \times 10}{500}  \\\\
a            &= \dfrac{\cancel{500} \times 2}{\cancel{500}}  \\\\
a            &= 2
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-890C9879-85CE-43C2-99E6-96A39CD6E6F7'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

What number (written in digits) is two hundred and twenty two
**less** than two thousand?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 2000 - 222 \\\\
&= 1778
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$1778$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
&= 2000 - 222 \\\\
&= 1778
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</code></pre>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

The tables in the Dining Hall can each seat $8$ pupils. 

How many tables are needed to seat $180$ pupils?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Tables needed}                          &= \dfrac {\text{Total pupils}} {\text{Pupil per table}} \\\\
                                              &= \dfrac {180} {8} \\\\
                                              &= \dfrac {45 \times 4} {2 \times 4} \\\\
                                              &= \dfrac {45 \times \cancel 4} {2 \times \cancel 4} \\\\
                                              &= \dfrac {45} {2} \\\\
                                              &= 22 \dfrac {1} {2} \\\\
                                              &= 23
\end{aligned}
$

Total tables needed $= 23$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$23 \ \text{tables}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Tables needed}                          &= \dfrac {\text{Total pupils}} {\text{Pupil per table}} \\\\
                                              &= \dfrac {180} {8} \\\\
                                              &= \dfrac {45 \times 4} {2 \times 4} \\\\
                                              &= \dfrac {45 \times \cancel 4} {2 \times \cancel 4} \\\\
                                              &= \dfrac {45} {2} \\\\
                                              &= 22 \dfrac {1} {2} \\\\
                                              &= 23
\end{aligned}
$

Total tables needed $= 23$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$23 \ \text{tables}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Jake paid a $\pounds 50$ deposit for a bike, then monthly
payments of $\pounds 25$ each. The bike cost $\pounds 275$. 

How many monthly payments did Jake have to make to buy it?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number of monthly payments $= m$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
50 + 25m                                      &= 275 \\\\
25m                                           &= 275 - 50 \\\\
25m                                           &= 225 \\\\
m                                             &= \dfrac {225} {25} \\\\
m                                             &= \dfrac {9 \times 25} {25} \\\\
m                                             &= \dfrac {9 \times \cancel{25}} {\cancel{25}} \\\\
m                                             &= 9
\end{aligned}
$

The number of monthly payments $= 9$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$9$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number of monthly payments $= m$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
50 + 25m                                      &= 275 \\\\
25m                                           &= 275 - 50 \\\\
25m                                           &= 225 \\\\
m                                             &= \dfrac {225} {25} \\\\
m                                             &= \dfrac {9 \times 25} {25} \\\\
m                                             &= \dfrac {9 \times \cancel{25}} {\cancel{25}} \\\\
m                                             &= 9
\end{aligned}
$

The number of monthly payments $= 9$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$9$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

There are $500$ pupils at Lily's school.

There are $30$ more girls than boys. 

How many girls are there?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number of girls $= g$

Number of boys $= g - 30$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of girls} + \text{Number of boys}       &= \text{Total pupils} \\\\
g + (g - 30)                                         &= 500 \\\\
2g                                                   &= 500 + 30 \\\\
2g                                                   &= 530 \\\\
g                                                    &= \dfrac {530} {2} \\\\
g                                                    &= \dfrac {2 \times 265} {2} \\\\
g                                                    &= \dfrac {\cancel{2} \times 265} {\cancel{2}} \\\\
g                                                    &= 265
\end{aligned}
$

Number of girls at Lily's school $= 265$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$265 \ \text{girls}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number of girls $= g$

Number of boys $= g - 30$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of girls} + \text{Number of boys}       &= \text{Total pupils} \\\\
g + (g - 30)                                         &= 500 \\\\
2g                                                   &= 500 + 30 \\\\
2g                                                   &= 530 \\\\
g                                                    &= \dfrac {530} {2} \\\\
g                                                    &= \dfrac {2 \times 265} {2} \\\\
g                                                    &= \dfrac {\cancel{2} \times 265} {\cancel{2}} \\\\
g                                                    &= 265
\end{aligned}
$

Number of girls at Lily's school $= 265$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$265 \ \text{girls}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

The sum of five consecutive whole numbers is $100$. 

What is the smallest of these five numbers?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the whole numbers $= a - 2, a - 1, a , a + 1, a + 2$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a - 2 + a - 1 + a + a + 1 + a + 2   &= 100 \\\\
a + a + a + a + a - 2 + 2 - 1 + 1   &= 100 \\\\
5a                                  &= 100 \\\\
a                                   &= \dfrac {100} {5} \\\\
a                                   &= \dfrac {20 \times 5} {5} \\\\
a                                   &= \dfrac {20 \times \cancel 5} {\cancel 5} \\\\
a                                   &= 20
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Smallest whole number}        &= a - 2 \\\\
                                    &= 20 - 2 \\\\
                                    &= 18
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$18$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the whole numbers $= a - 2, a - 1, a , a + 1, a + 2$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a - 2 + a - 1 + a + a + 1 + a + 2   &= 100 \\\\
a + a + a + a + a - 2 + 2 - 1 + 1   &= 100 \\\\
5a                                  &= 100 \\\\
a                                   &= \dfrac {100} {5} \\\\
a                                   &= \dfrac {20 \times 5} {5} \\\\
a                                   &= \dfrac {20 \times \cancel 5} {\cancel 5} \\\\
a                                   &= 20
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Smallest whole number}        &= a - 2 \\\\
                                    &= 20 - 2 \\\\
                                    &= 18
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$18$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

The sum of two numbers is $22$ and their difference is $4$. 

What are the two numbers?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the two numbers $= a, b$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a + b       &= 22 \\\\
a - b       &= 4
\end{aligned}
$

Let's add both the equations.

$
\begin{aligned}
a + b + a - b           &= 22 + 4 \\\\
a + a + b - b           &= 22 + 4 \\\\
2a                      &= 26 \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {2 \times 13} {2} \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 13} {\cancel 2} \\\\
a                       &= 13 \\\\
a + b                   &= 22 \\\\
b                       &= 22 - a \\\\
b                       &= 22 - 13 \\\\
b                       &= 9
\end{aligned}
$

The two numbers $= 13, 9$.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$13, 9$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the two numbers $= a, b$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a + b       &= 22 \\\\
a - b       &= 4
\end{aligned}
$

Let's add both the equations.

$
\begin{aligned}
a + b + a - b           &= 22 + 4 \\\\
a + a + b - b           &= 22 + 4 \\\\
2a                      &= 26 \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {2 \times 13} {2} \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 13} {\cancel 2} \\\\
a                       &= 13 \\\\
a + b                   &= 22 \\\\
b                       &= 22 - a \\\\
b                       &= 22 - 13 \\\\
b                       &= 9
\end{aligned}
$

The two numbers $= 13, 9$.
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$13, 9$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-7DED225D-BE66-49EA-A9DA-91175FD26034'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

It is $165 \ \text{minutes}$ before midnight. 
Put one digit into each of the four boxes to show the time on this $24$-hour digital clock.


$
\huge {
\boxed {\phantom{2}} \ 
\boxed {\phantom{2}}
{:} 
\boxed {\phantom{1}} \ 
\boxed {\phantom{3}}
}
$


</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 24{:}00 - 165 \ \text {minutes} \\\\
&= 24{:}00 - 120 \ \text {minutes} - 45 \ \text {minutes} \\\\
&= 24{:}00 - 2 \ \text {hours} \ 45 \ \text {minutes} \\\\
&= 21{:}15
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$21{:}15$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
&= 24{:}00 - 165 \ \text {minutes} \\\\
&= 24{:}00 - 120 \ \text {minutes} - 45 \ \text {minutes} \\\\
&= 24{:}00 - 2 \ \text {hours} \ 45 \ \text {minutes} \\\\
&= 21{:}15
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$21{:}15$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

There is some liquid in the container.

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 360 \div 1000 \ \text{ml} \\\\
&= \dfrac{360}{1000} \ \text{ml} \\\\
&= \dfrac{36\cancel{0}}{100\cancel{0}} \ \text{ml} \\\\
&= \dfrac{36}{100} \ \text{ml} \\\\
&= 0.36 \ \text{ml}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Calculate $50 \%$ of $20 \%$ of $6000$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 50 \% \ \text{of} \ 20 \% \ \text{of} \ 6000 \\\\
&= \dfrac {50} {100} \times \dfrac {20} {100} \times 6000 \\\\
&= \dfrac {50 \times 20 \times 6000} {100 \times 100} \\\\
&= \dfrac {5 \cancel 0 \times 2 \cancel 0 \times 60 \cancel {00}} {1 \cancel 0 \cancel 0 \times 1 \cancel {00}} \\\\
&= 5 \times 2 \times 60 \\\\
&= 10 \times 60 \\\\
&= 600
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$600$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
&= 50 \% \ \text{of} \ 20 \% \ \text{of} \ 6000 \\\\
&= \dfrac {50} {100} \times \dfrac {20} {100} \times 6000 \\\\
&= \dfrac {50 \times 20 \times 6000} {100 \times 100} \\\\
&= \dfrac {5 \cancel 0 \times 2 \cancel 0 \times 60 \cancel {00}} {1 \cancel 0 \cancel 0 \times 1 \cancel {00}} \\\\
&= 5 \times 2 \times 60 \\\\
&= 10 \times 60 \\\\
&= 600
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

&= 20172017 \div 2017 \\\\

&= 10001

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Required value}           &= (49 \times 57) + 7 \\\\
                                &= (49 \times (2793 \div 49)) + 7 \\\\
                                &= \left( 49 \times \dfrac {2793} {49} \right) + 7 \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {49 \times 2793} {49} + 7 \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {\cancel {49} \times 2793} {\cancel {49}} + 7 \\\\
                                &= 2793 + 7 \\\\
                                &= 2800
\end{aligned}
$

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-EB2674B4-189E-4F68-A98E-E3E6353FB1E6'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

At Ali's shop, a multipack of $8$ toilet rolls costs $\pounds 2.40$. 

At Mya's shop, the cost per toilet roll is twice as much. 

How much is a pack of $3$ toilet rolls at Mya's shop?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Cost per toilet roll at Ali's shop}       &= \pounds \dfrac {2.4} {8} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {24} {8 \times 10} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {8 \times 3} {8 \times 10} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {\cancel 8 \times 3} {\cancel 8 \times 10} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {3} {10} \\\\
                                                &= \pounds 0.3
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Cost per toilet roll at Mya's shop}       &= \pounds 0.3 \times 2 \\\\
                                                &= \pounds 0.6
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Cost of 3 toilet rolls at Mya's shop}        &= \pounds 0.6 \times 3 \\\\
                                                  &= \pounds 1.8
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 1.8$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Cost per toilet roll at Ali's shop}       &= \pounds \dfrac {2.4} {8} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {24} {8 \times 10} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {8 \times 3} {8 \times 10} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {\cancel 8 \times 3} {\cancel 8 \times 10} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {3} {10} \\\\
                                                &= \pounds 0.3
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Cost per toilet roll at Mya's shop}       &= \pounds 0.3 \times 2 \\\\
                                                &= \pounds 0.6
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Cost of 3 toilet rolls at Mya's shop}        &= \pounds 0.6 \times 3 \\\\
                                                  &= \pounds 1.8
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\pounds 1.8$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-FCE748F5-1F9C-4B3F-9062-3C57657E02CB'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Each number in a sequence is calculated by multiplying the previous number by $100$ then adding $1$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

%empty%

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

%empty%

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

If the first number in the sequence is $0$, what is the fourth number?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the question,

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Second number}   &= 0 \times 100 + 1 \\\\
                        &= 0 + 1 \\\\
                        &= 1 \\\\
\text {Third number}    &= 1 \times 100 + 1 \\\\
                        &= 100 + 1 \\\\
                        &= 101 \\\\
\text {Fourth number}   &= 101 \times 100 + 1 \\\\
                        &= 10100 + 1 \\\\
                        &= 10101
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$10101$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>As per the question,

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Second number}   &= 0 \times 100 + 1 \\\\
                        &= 0 + 1 \\\\
                        &= 1 \\\\
\text {Third number}    &= 1 \times 100 + 1 \\\\
                        &= 100 + 1 \\\\
                        &= 101 \\\\
\text {Fourth number}   &= 101 \times 100 + 1 \\\\
                        &= 10100 + 1 \\\\
                        &= 10101
\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$10101$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

If the second number in the sequence is $51$, what is the first number?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the first number $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Second number}   &= 51 \\\\
a \times 100 + 1        &= 51 \\\\
a \times 100            &= 51 - 1 \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {50} {100} \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {50} {2 \times 50} \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {\cancel {50}} {2 \times \cancel {50}} \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {1} {2}
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\dfrac {1} {2}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the first number $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Second number}   &= 51 \\\\
a \times 100 + 1        &= 51 \\\\
a \times 100            &= 51 - 1 \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {50} {100} \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {50} {2 \times 50} \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {\cancel {50}} {2 \times \cancel {50}} \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {1} {2}
\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\dfrac {1} {2}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
</ul>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-F24C6B36-9D77-42D8-9DEC-C5CBFC10D161'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

A sequence of numbers is formed by always adding the same number
to get the next number in the sequence.

The $10 \text{th}$ number in the sequence is $23$ and the $15 \text{th}$ number is $38$.

What is the $12 \text{th}$ number?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the question,

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Difference}      &= \dfrac {38 - 23} {5} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {15} {5} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {3 \times 5} {5} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {3 \times \cancel {5}} {\cancel {5}} \\\\
                        &= 3 \\\\
12 \ \text {th number}  &= 23 + 3 + 3 \\\\
                        &= 29
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$29$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>As per the question,

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Difference}      &= \dfrac {38 - 23} {5} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {15} {5} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {3 \times 5} {5} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {3 \times \cancel {5}} {\cancel {5}} \\\\
                        &= 3 \\\\
12 \ \text {th number}  &= 23 + 3 + 3 \\\\
                        &= 29
\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$29$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-554EE914-E3B3-4005-88CF-95A6A1AB7B12'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Billy is $2 \ \text{metres}$ and $2 \ \text{centimetres}$ tall. Write his height as a decimal, in metres.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Height}   &= 2 \ \text{ metres } 2 \ \text{ centimetres } \\\\
                &= 2 \ \text{ metres } + \dfrac{2}{100} \ \text{metres} \\\\
                &= 2 \ \text{ metres } + 0.02 \ \text{metres} \\\\
                &= 2.02 \ \text{metres}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$2.02 \ \text{metres}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Height}   &= 2 \ \text{ metres } 2 \ \text{ centimetres } \\\\
                &= 2 \ \text{ metres } + \dfrac{2}{100} \ \text{metres} \\\\
                &= 2 \ \text{ metres } + 0.02 \ \text{metres} \\\\
                &= 2.02 \ \text{metres}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$2.02 \ \text{metres}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

The diagram shows a cross made from square tiles. 

The area of the cross is $245 \ \text{cm}^2$.

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


</div>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What is the area of one tile?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the diagram,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Area of one tile}            &= \dfrac {\text {Area of the cross}} {\text {Number of tiles}} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {245} {5} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {49 \times 5} {5} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {49 \times \cancel {5}} {\cancel {5}} \\\\
                                    &= 49 \ \text {cm}^2
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$49 \ \text {cm}^2$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>As per the diagram,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Area of one tile}            &= \dfrac {\text {Area of the cross}} {\text {Number of tiles}} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {245} {5} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {49 \times 5} {5} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {49 \times \cancel {5}} {\cancel {5}} \\\\
                                    &= 49 \ \text {cm}^2
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$49 \ \text {cm}^2$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What is the length of one side of a tile?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Area of square} = \text{side} \times \text{side}$


Let the length of one side of tile $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a \times a          &= 49 \\\\
a^2                 &= 49 \\\\
a                   &= \sqrt {49} \\\\
a                   &= 7 \ \text {cm}
\end{aligned}
$

Length of one side of tile $= 7 \ \text {cm}$.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$7 \ \text {cm}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_area_square_expanded

$
\begin{aligned}

a^2                 &= 49 \\\\
a                   &= \sqrt {49} \\\\
a                   &= 7 \ \text {cm}
\end{aligned}
$

Length of one side of tile $= 7 \ \text {cm}$.
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$7 \ \text {cm}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What is the perimeter of the cross?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Perimeter of polygon} = \text{Sum of all sides}$


*TODO: ADD an explanation to count total number of sides in the shape is 12*.

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Perimeter}       &= \text {Total sides} \times \text {Length of one side} \\\\
                        &= 12 \times 7 \\\\
                        &= 84 \ \text {cm}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$84 \ \text {cm}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_expanded_perimeter_polygon

</div>
</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Buildings take up $\dfrac{3}{5}$ of the area. How much area is this?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Area of Building}            &= \dfrac {3} {5} \times \text {Area of site} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {3} {5} \times 8000 \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {3 \times 8000} {5} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {3 \times 5 \times 1600} {5} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {3 \times \cancel {5} \times 1600} {\cancel {5}} \\\\
                                    &= 3 \times 1600 \\\\
                                    &= 4800 \ \text {cm}^2
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$4800 \ \text {cm}^2$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

                                    &= \dfrac {3} {5} \times 8000 \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {3 \times 8000} {5} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {3 \times 5 \times 1600} {5} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {3 \times \cancel {5} \times 1600} {\cancel {5}} \\\\
                                    &= 3 \times 1600 \\\\
                                    &= 4800 \ \text {cm}^2
\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

$

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

The area of the grass is seven times the area of the footpaths.

What area is covered in grass?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the area of the footpath $= a$

Area of the grass $= 7a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Area of grass} + \text {Area of footpath}    &= 1200 \\\\
7a + a                                              &= 1200 \\\\
8a                                                  &= 1200 \\\\
a                                                   &= \dfrac {1200} {8} \\\\
a                                                   &= \dfrac {8 \times 150} {8} \\\\
a                                                   &= \dfrac {\cancel {8} \times 150} {\cancel {8}} \\\\
a                                                   &= 150 \ \text {cm}^2
\end {aligned}
$

Let's find the area of the grass.

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Area of the grass}   &= 7 \times a \\\\
                            &= 7 \times 150 \\\\
                            &= 1050 \ \text {cm}^2
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$1050 \ \text {cm}^2$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the area of the footpath $= a$

Area of the grass $= 7a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Area of grass} + \text {Area of footpath}    &= 1200 \\\\
7a + a                                              &= 1200 \\\\
8a                                                  &= 1200 \\\\
a                                                   &= \dfrac {1200} {8} \\\\
a                                                   &= \dfrac {8 \times 150} {8} \\\\
a                                                   &= \dfrac {\cancel {8} \times 150} {\cancel {8}} \\\\
a                                                   &= 150 \ \text {cm}^2
\end {aligned}
$

Let's find the area of the grass.

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Area of the grass}   &= 7 \times a \\\\
                            &= 7 \times 150 \\\\
                            &= 1050 \ \text {cm}^2
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$1050 \ \text {cm}^2$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-173F0773-5ABA-45CB-8EB7-AEE368A5D195'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

The diagram shows the coordinates of three points $A, B$ and $C$. 

Shape $ABCD$ is a rectangle.

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


What are the coordinates of point D?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the graph,

The x-axis for point A and D is same.

Similarly, The y-axis for point C and D are same.

Hence, the coordinates of point D $= (2, 1)$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$(2, 1)$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>As per the graph,

The x-axis for point A and D is same.

Similarly, The y-axis for point C and D are same.

Hence, the coordinates of point D $= (2, 1)$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$(2, 1)$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-CF4511CA-E39F-47E2-A905-DD305EB0E624'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Each of the four different symbols stands for a number. 

Work out the number that each symbol stands for.

$\spadesuit + \spadesuit + \spadesuit = 27$

$\spadesuit + \spadesuit = 26$

$\spadesuit - \spadesuit = 9$ 

$\spadesuit + \spadesuit + \spadesuit = \spadesuit$ 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
9 + 9 + 9 &= 27 \\\\
13 + 13 &= 26 \\\\
18 - 9 &= 9 \\\\
2 + 9 + 2 &= 13
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$9, 13, 18, 2$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
9 + 9 + 9 &= 27 \\\\
13 + 13 &= 26 \\\\
18 - 9 &= 9 \\\\
2 + 9 + 2 &= 13
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$9, 13, 18, 2$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>


</div>
</li>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-241B7016-CCDD-4772-8E08-6AB8F115D0BE'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Toby is given four bags of fruit. 

Bag $A$ contains $2$ apples, $4$ oranges and $3$ pears. 

Bag $B$ contains $4$ apples, $5$ oranges and $3$ pears. 

Bag $C$ contains $3$ apples, $6$ oranges and $4$ pears. 

Bag $D$ contains $5$ apples, $4$ oranges and $9$ pears.

Toby is allowed to chose one bag and then he is 
given one piece of fruit from that bag at random.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

%empty%

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

%empty%

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Which bag should Toby choose to have the **greatest** chance
of getting an apple?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let's calculate the probability for each bag of getting an apple,

For bag A:

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Probability of getting an apple} &= \dfrac {\text {Favourable outcomes}} {\text {Total possible outcomes}} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {2} {2 + 4 + 3} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {2} {9}
\end {aligned}
$

For bag B:

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Probability of getting an apple} &= \dfrac {\text {Favourable outcomes}} {\text {Total possible outcomes}} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {4} {4 + 5 + 3} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {4} {12} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {4} {3 \times 4} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {4} {3 \times \cancel {4}} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {1} {3}
\end {aligned}
$

For bag C:

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Probability of getting an apple} &= \dfrac {\text {Favourable outcomes}} {\text {Total possible outcomes}} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {3} {3 + 6 + 4} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {3} {13}
\end {aligned}
$

For bag D:

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Probability of getting an apple} &= \dfrac {\text {Favourable outcomes}} {\text {Total possible outcomes}} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {5} {5 + 4 + 9} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {5} {18}
\end {aligned}
$

Now let's arrange the above calcuations in ascending order:

$\text {Bag A}, \quad \text {Bag B}, \quad \text {Bag C}, \quad \text {Bag D}$

$\dfrac {2} {9}, \quad \dfrac {1} {3}, \quad \dfrac {3} {13}, \quad \dfrac {5} {18}$

$\dfrac {2 \times 26} {9 \times 26}, \quad \dfrac {1 \times 78} {3 \times 78}, \quad \dfrac {3 \times 18} {13 \times 18}, \quad \dfrac {5 \times 13} {18 \times 13}$

$\dfrac {52} {234}, \quad \dfrac {78} {234}, \quad \dfrac {54} {234}, \quad \dfrac {65} {234}$

$\dfrac {52} {234}, \quad \dfrac {54} {234}, \quad \dfrac {65} {234}, \quad \dfrac {78} {234}$

Hence, Bag B has the highest probability of getting an apple.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\text {Bag B}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let's calculate the probability for each bag of getting an apple,

$

                                        &= \dfrac {2} {2 + 4 + 3} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {2} {9}
\end {aligned}
$

$

                                        &= \dfrac {4} {4 + 5 + 3} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {4} {12} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {4} {3 \times 4} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {4} {3 \times \cancel {4}} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {1} {3}
\end {aligned}
$

                                        &= \dfrac {3} {3 + 6 + 4} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {3} {13}
\end {aligned}
$

For bag D:

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Probability of getting an apple} &= \dfrac {\text {Favourable outcomes}} {\text {Total possible outcomes}} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {5} {5 + 4 + 9} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {5} {18}
\end {aligned}
$

Now let's arrange the above calcuations in ascending order:

</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-0BAEBC7B-30E9-4C81-8BF3-35BE82D1301D'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

A rectangular garden is surrounded by a path of a fixed width.

The perimeter of the garden is $24 \ \text{metres}$ **less** than the 
distance along the outside edge of the path.

What is the width of the path?

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Perimeter of rectangle} = 2 \times (\text{length} + \text{width})$


Let the width of the path $= a$

Let the length of the garden $= l$

Let the width of the garden $= w$

Length of outside edge of the path $= l + a$

Width of outside edge of the path $= w + a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
2 \times ((l + a) + (w + a)) - 24   &= 2 \times (l + w) \\\\
2 \times (l + w + 2a) - 24          &= 2 \times (l + w) \\\\
2l + 2w + 4a - 24                   &= 2l + 2w \\\\
4a                                  &= 2l + 2w - 2l -  2w + 24 \\\\
4a                                  &= 24 \\\\
a                                   &= \dfrac {24} {4} \\\\
a                                   &= \dfrac {4 \times 6} {4} \\\\
a                                   &= \dfrac {\cancel {4} \times 6} {\cancel {4}} \\\\
a                                   &= 6 \ \text {cm}
\end{aligned}
$

Width of the path $= 6 \ \text {cm}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$6 \ \text {cm}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_expanded_perimeter_rectangle

Let the width of the path $= a$

Let the length of the garden $= l$

Let the width of the garden $= w$

Length of outside edge of the path $= l + a$

Width of outside edge of the path $= w + a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
2 \times ((l + a) + (w + a)) - 24   &= 2 \times (l + w) \\\\
2 \times (l + w + 2a) - 24          &= 2 \times (l + w) \\\\
2l + 2w + 4a - 24                   &= 2l + 2w \\\\
4a                                  &= 2l + 2w - 2l -  2w + 24 \\\\
4a                                  &= 24 \\\\
a                                   &= \dfrac {24} {4} \\\\
a                                   &= \dfrac {4 \times 6} {4} \\\\
a                                   &= \dfrac {\cancel {4} \times 6} {\cancel {4}} \\\\
a                                   &= 6 \ \text {cm}
\end{aligned}
$

Width of the path $= 6 \ \text {cm}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$6 \ \text {cm}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

Granny takes one a day of each of two types of tablet.

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What is the product of the smallest two-digit prime number and the largest two-digit square number?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Smallest two-digit prime number} &= 11 \\\\
\text{Largest two-digit square number} &= 81 \\\\
11 \times 81 &= 891 
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$891$

</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-8FD4F287-E8F1-42F2-B909-FC3B822573A4'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Paddy is leading in a race. 

He is $81 \ \text{metres}$ in front of Sam who is in last place. 

Martin is between Paddy and Sam. 

Martin is $40 \ \text{metres}$ from the finish line and is twice as far away from Sam as he is from Paddy. 

How far has Sam left to run?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let distance of Paddy from finish line $= a$

then distance of Sam from finish line $= a + 81$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a + 81 - 40             &= 2 \times (40 - a) \\\\
a + 41                  &= 80 - 2a \\\\
a + 2a                  &= 80 - 41 \\\\
3a                      &= 39 \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {39} {3} \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {3 \times 13} {3} \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {\cancel 3 \times 13} { \cancel 3} \\\\
a                       &= 13
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Distance of Sam from the finish line}     &= (13 + 81) \ \text{metres} \\\\
                                                &= 94 \ \text{metres}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$94 \ \text{metres}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let distance of Paddy from finish line $= a$

then distance of Sam from finish line $= a + 81$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a + 81 - 40             &= 2 \times (40 - a) \\\\
a + 41                  &= 80 - 2a \\\\
a + 2a                  &= 80 - 41 \\\\
3a                      &= 39 \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {39} {3} \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {3 \times 13} {3} \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {\cancel 3 \times 13} { \cancel 3} \\\\
a                       &= 13
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Distance of Sam from the finish line}     &= (13 + 81) \ \text{metres} \\\\
                                                &= 94 \ \text{metres}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$94 \ \text{metres}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-BD782688-6E8D-4514-AA56-D01E50FF22E7'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Mr Patel and his sons Humza and Kian have the same birthday.

Today, Mr Patel is $34$, Humza is $6$ and Kian is $3$. 

How old will they be when Mr Patel's age is the sum of his sons' ages?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number of years ahead $= a$

Mr Patel's age $= 34 + a$

Humza's age $= 6 + a$

Kian's age $= 3 + a$

As per the question,

$
\begin {aligned}
34 + a                  &= 6 + a + 3 + a \\\\
34 + a                  &= 9 + 2a \\\\
2a + 9                  &= 34 + a \\\\
2a - a                  &= 34 - 9 \\\\
a                       &= 25 \ \text {years} \\\\
\text{Mr Patel's age}   &= 34 + a \\\\
                        &= 34 + 25  \\\\
                        &= 59 \ \text {years} \\\\
\text{Hamza's age}      &= 6 + a \\\\
                        &= 6 + 25  \\\\
                        &= 31 \ \text {years} \\\\
\text{Kian's age}       &= 3 + a \\\\
                        &= 3 + 25  \\\\
                        &= 28 \ \text {years}
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

Mr Patel $=$ $59 \ \text {years}$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

Hamza $=$ $31 \ \text {years}$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

Kian $=$ $28 \ \text {years}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number of years ahead $= a$

Mr Patel's age $= 34 + a$

Humza's age $= 6 + a$

Kian's age $= 3 + a$

As per the question,

$
\begin {aligned}
34 + a                  &= 6 + a + 3 + a \\\\
34 + a                  &= 9 + 2a \\\\
2a + 9                  &= 34 + a \\\\
2a - a                  &= 34 - 9 \\\\
a                       &= 25 \ \text {years} \\\\
\text{Mr Patel's age}   &= 34 + a \\\\
                        &= 34 + 25  \\\\
                        &= 59 \ \text {years} \\\\
\text{Hamza's age}      &= 6 + a \\\\
                        &= 6 + 25  \\\\
                        &= 31 \ \text {years} \\\\
\text{Kian's age}       &= 3 + a \\\\
                        &= 3 + 25  \\\\
                        &= 28 \ \text {years}
\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Mr Patel</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$59 \ \text {years}$
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

</div>

</code></pre>
</div>
